metadata:
  visible_in_settings: true
connector: walmart
display_name: Walmart
icon: walmart
description: Search Walmart, ask Sparky and build a Walmart cart
request_quota:
  mode: shadow
  workers: [walmart]
  # Walmart allows 30 requests per minute per MCP session.
  queries_per_minute: 30
  burst: 10
cli:
  binary: walmart
  skill: walmart
auth:
  provider: walmart
  required: true
  mcp:
    protocol_version: "2025-06-18"
    cagi:
      path: /sparky-mcp
      # The cart, the linked Walmart account and Sparky's conversation live on
      # the MCP session, so each user keeps one.
      persistent_session: true
    tool_names:
      shopping.read:
        - search_products
        - get_product_details
        - get_cart
        - get_mandate
        - link_account
      cart.prepare:
        - update_cart
        - remove_item_from_cart
      # Sparky is Walmart's own agent: a message can do anything Sparky can,
      # including changing the user's walmart.com cart, so it is a write and
      # runs alone.
      agent.message:
        - ask_sparky
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    response_guard: verification_codes
    methods:
      shopping.read:
        label: Search products and view your cart
        approval_phrase: search Walmart and view your cart
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write permissions
    response_guard: verification_codes
    methods:
      cart.prepare:
        default: allow
        label: Edit your Walmart cart
        approval_phrase: edit your Walmart cart
        approval_buttons:
          allow_once: "Update cart"
          deny: "Don't update"
          deny.task: "Don't update"
      agent.message:
        default: allow
        label: Talk to Sparky, Walmart's AI agent, which can act on your cart and account
        approval_phrase: send a message to Sparky, Walmart's AI agent, which can act on your Walmart cart and account
consent_screen:
  bullets:
    - icon: data_types
      title: "Shop with Walmart"
      text: "Muse can search Walmart, compare products and build a cart for you."
    - icon: info
      title: "Muse doesn't place orders"
      text: "When you're ready to buy, open the product on Walmart and check out there."
    - icon: caution
      title: "Check before you buy"
      text: "Prices and availability can change. Check them on Walmart before you buy."
  footer_paragraphs:
    - "The info used for your tasks is part of your interactions with Muse, which we may use to improve AI at Meta. You can manage this in Settings. [Learn more](https://muse.ai/privacy)"
    - "Muse will exchange info with Walmart. To learn more, view their [terms](https://www.walmart.com/help/article/walmart-com-terms-of-use/3b75080af40340d6bbd596f116fae5a0) and [privacy policy](https://corporate.walmart.com/privacy-security/walmart-privacy-notice)."
browserversion: 1
metadata:
  visible_in_settings: true
auth: none
connector: browser
display_name: Browser
description: >-
  This is a built-in connector that allows Muse to search the web and open
  websites.
icon: browser
# Browser exposes only the five user-configurable permission methods below.
# Other browser behavior is governed by its dedicated safety checks, where
# applicable, and does not participate in connector-method policy.
# Checkout detection remains a targeted fresh-consent contract outside this
# manifest and never creates reusable Browser connector policy.
actions:
  read:
    default: ask
    label: Read permissions
    # Authenticated foreground and trusted product-context HTTP(S) goto use the
    # screened runtime Ask replacement. Other origins retain the connector
    # prompt.
    methods:
      page.goto:
        label: Visit websites
        approval_phrase: open this website using Browser
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write and delete permissions
    methods:
      page.download:
        label: Download files
        approval_phrase: download this file using Browser
      page.submit:
        # A task pre-approval of one order answers the checkout's payment
        # approval, checked against the verified quote; ordinary submits carry
        # no binding and never consume it.
        preapproval: purchase
        # STOPGAP (T278621935): auto-allow ordinary form submits until
        # high-risk submit classification can distinguish consequential
        # actions. This is a per-method override; the group remains Ask for
        # future methods. Checkout detection independently uses mandatory
        # fresh consent outside this user-configurable manifest.
        default: allow
        label: Submit a form or POST request
      page.upload:
        # Every upload asks. The card offers Allow once and Deny, plus "Always
        # allow this site" in the owner's chat and scheduled tasks. That
        # option saves an exact-origin (scheme, host, port) grant for uploads
        # only; Settings stays Ask/Deny and there is no method-wide or global
        # Allow. Only browser page.upload may declare this policy
        # (hatch-sentinel/docs/approvals.md).
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_site_allow
        label: Upload files
        approval_phrase: upload this file using Browser
      page.credential_fill:
        label: Fill saved credentials

display_name: Glasses
description: >-
  This is a built-in connector that governs consequential actions Muse takes
  through a paired device on your behalf, such as placing a call or sending a
  message when you ask from your glasses.
# Reviewed capabilities Hatch asks about, not the device's command catalog.
# See docs/hatch-security-architecture.md §5.3.10.
actions:
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Calls and messages
    methods:
      call.place:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Place phone calls
        approval_phrase: place this call
      # Renaming a method also requires updating `GATED_DEVICE_COMMANDS`
      # (hatch-sentinel-config/src/device_commands.rs).
      message.send:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Send messages
        approval_phrase: send this message

metadata:
  visible_in_settings: false
auth: none
connector: shopify-ucp
display_name: Shopify UCP
icon: shopify
description: Assemble a cart, prepare payment, view checkout, complete checkout, and cancel Shopify UCP orders for in-chat purchases.
cli:
  binary: shopify-ucp-cli
  skill: shopify_ucp
cli_subcommands:
  - checkout get
  - checkout create
  - checkout update
  - checkout complete
  - checkout cancel
  - order get
  - cart create
  - cart get
  - cart update
  - cart cancel
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Order permissions
    methods:
      checkout.get:
        label: View checkout
      order.details:
        label: View order
      cart.get:
        label: View cart
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Purchase permissions
    methods:
      checkout.create:
        # Agent-internal: minting a session has no user-visible side effect
        # (no money moves, no inventory hold) and the user has nothing to
        # decide yet.
        default: allow
        label: Create checkout
      checkout.update:
        # Delivery selection only. Buyer data and discount-code replacement
        # have their own methods below, each checked independently.
        default: allow
        label: Update checkout delivery
      checkout.buyer.update:
        # Buyer details can be filled after checkout creation without adding a
        # second approval to the active checkout flow.
        default: allow
        label: Update checkout buyer information
      checkout.discounts.update:
        # Applying codes the user asked for should not add another approval to
        # the active checkout flow; it is still checked independently.
        default: allow
        label: Update checkout discounts
        approval_phrase: update discount codes on a Shopify checkout
      checkout.complete:
        # The final checkout call consumes the payment credential prepared
        # after the selected wallet's explicit payment-method approval. The
        # wallet owns the user HITL, so Shopify completion must not prompt again.
        default: allow
        label: Place order
      checkout.cancel:
        # Cancellation reverses or stops a checkout/order and does not create a
        # new spend. Treat it like quote updates: no extra HITL prompt.
        default: allow
        label: Cancel order
      cart.create:
        # A cart moves no money and holds no inventory; the purchase HITL is
        # downstream at `checkout complete`. Nothing for the user to decide yet.
        default: allow
        label: Create cart
      cart.update:
        # Full-replacement cart edit. Same rationale as create: pre-purchase,
        # no spend, no inventory hold.
        default: allow
        label: Update cart
      cart.cancel:
        # Cancelling a cart stops a pre-purchase draft and creates no spend.
        default: allow
        label: Cancel cart
commands:
  "checkout get": checkout.get
  "checkout create": checkout.create
  "checkout update": checkout.update
  "checkout complete": checkout.complete
  "checkout cancel": checkout.cancel
  "order get": order.details
  "cart create": cart.create
  "cart get": cart.get
  "cart update": cart.update
  "cart cancel": cart.cancel

# Valid CLI subcommand paths, consumed by jarvis runtime-event logging to enrich
# tool-variant telemetry (e.g. `facebook-cli/marketplace/search`). Source of
# truth for this list; the `subcommand_paths_match_manifest` test in the
# facebook-cli crate keeps it in lockstep with the clap subcommand tree.
#
# The list tracks the `prod` build flavor in Cargo.toml, which is what ships.
# Commands that exist in source but are gated out of `prod` do not belong here:
# a path the shipped binary cannot run is a phantom telemetry variant that can
# never fire, and it makes a zero row read as "unused" when it means "absent".
#
# Connector lifecycle commands are intentionally excluded: status is surfaced by
# the daemon, and connect-url / disconnect-url are credential-free URL helpers
# handled by the binary entrypoint before clap parsing.

# Drafts are a DATA CLASS, not an API surface: a Gmail draft is an ordinary
# message carrying the system DRAFT label, so message and thread reads return
# full draft content even while the dedicated drafts methods are denied.
# Declaring the class makes Sentinel attach an exclusion constraint to every
# allowed messages read while "Access drafts" is deny/ask; the trusted
# hatch_gws_cli worker enforces it (query rewrite on list surfaces, DRAFT
# labelId filtering on reads) and fails closed on constraints it does not
# implement. Aliased raw methods (users.threads.*, users.history.list,
# users.messages.attachments.get) authorize as these two canonical keys, so
# listing the keys covers every aliased spelling.
data_classes:
  drafts:
    governed_by: users.drafts.list
    emitted_by:
      - users.messages.list
      - users.messages.get

# A freeBusy query returns the start/end of every event in the window — the
# complete timing skeleton of the schedule "Access events" governs — so it is
# an event-schedule DATA CLASS emitter. While "Access events" is deny/ask,
# Sentinel attaches an exclusion constraint to allowed free_busy.query calls
# and the trusted hatch_gws_cli worker refuses them (freeBusy has no partial
# projection to filter: its entire response is the class).

apple_healthkitversion: 1
metadata:
  visible_in_settings: false
auth: none
cli:
  binary: health-cli
  skill: apple_healthkit
connector: apple_healthkit
category: health
display_name: Apple Health
icon: apple_healthkit
description: Read synced Apple Health data (daily metrics, sleep, workouts).
# Connector id MUST equal this skill's `name` ("apple_healthkit") so the daemon
# associates the manifest with the skill and runtime CLI-subcommand lookup
# (keyed by skill name). This connector intentionally remains omitted from
# Settings.
#
# Valid CLI subcommand paths, consumed by jarvis runtime-event logging to enrich
# tool-variant telemetry (e.g. `health-cli/query/metrics`). Keep in lockstep
# with the clap subcommand tree in the health-cli crate. Paths are not
# provider-qualified: `--provider` selects the data source, not the subcommand.
#
# `delete` is intentionally excluded, and NOT an oversight to copy. This list is
# keyed by the skill jarvis attributes an invocation to, and a delete is
# attributed to the `health` skill (`health_cli_skill_name_for_args`), never to
# this provider — deliberately, so an irreversible erase is not filed under a
# read-only skill. `health` has no connector manifest to carry a list, so an
# entry here would never be read. Adding one back would look like it restored
# telemetry for deletion while changing nothing. Deletion is separately visible:
# the daemon emits a terminal row on /health-data/delete and Sentinel records a
# connector_authorization row naming `healthkit.delete`.
cli_subcommands:
  - "query metrics"
  - "query samples"
  - "query sessions"
  - "status"
  - "auth connect"
  - "auth disconnect"
# This connector is granted independently of every other health data source:
# Sentinel stores a grant against `(connector, action_key)`, and
# `connector_policy_method` in health-cli `src/lib.rs` selects this connector
# from `--provider healthkit`.
#
# The group key deliberately does NOT end in `_read`, and is not `read`.
# `connector_group_is_read_class` matches exactly those, and for a read-class
# group Sentinel overloads an `allow_once` approval into a turn-scoped grant
# instead of a single-use one. This key stays non-read-class so an approval
# remains single-use, exactly as the previous `health_cli` `healthkit` group
# behaved. Renaming it would be a prompting-frequency change, which does not
# belong in this change.
actions:
  healthkit:
    default: allow
    label: Apple Health
    methods:
      healthkit.query:
        label: Read daily metrics, sleep, and workouts from Apple Health
      healthkit.status:
        label: Check Apple Health sync status
  delete:

# Connector id MUST equal this skill's `name` ("google_health_connect") so the
# daemon associates the manifest with the skill and runtime CLI-subcommand
# lookup (keyed by skill name). This connector intentionally remains omitted
# from Settings.
#
# Valid CLI subcommand paths, consumed by jarvis runtime-event logging to enrich
# tool-variant telemetry (e.g. `health-cli/query/metrics`). Keep in lockstep
# with the clap subcommand tree in the health-cli crate. Paths are not
# provider-qualified: `--provider` selects the data source, not the subcommand.
#
# `delete` is intentionally excluded, and NOT an oversight to copy. This list is
# keyed by the skill jarvis attributes an invocation to, and a delete is
# attributed to the `health` skill (`health_cli_skill_name_for_args`), never to
# this provider — deliberately, so an irreversible erase is not filed under a
# read-only skill. `health` has no connector manifest to carry a list, so an
# entry here would never be read. Adding one back would look like it restored
# telemetry for deletion while changing nothing. Deletion is separately visible:
# the daemon emits a terminal row on /health-data/delete and Sentinel records a
# connector_authorization row naming `healthconnect.delete`.
cli_subcommands:
  - "query metrics"
  - "query samples"
  - "query sessions"
  - "status"
  - "auth connect"
  - "auth disconnect"
# This connector is granted independently of every other health data source:
# Sentinel stores a grant against `(connector, action_key)`, and
# `connector_policy_method` in health-cli `src/lib.rs` selects this connector
# from `--provider healthconnect`.
#
# The group key deliberately does NOT end in `_read`, and is not `read`.
# `connector_group_is_read_class` matches exactly those, and for a read-class
# group Sentinel overloads an `allow_once` approval into a turn-scoped grant
# instead of a single-use one. This key stays non-read-class so an approval
# remains single-use, exactly as the previous `health_cli` `healthconnect`
# group behaved. Renaming it would be a prompting-frequency change, which does
# not belong in this change.
actions:
  healthconnect:
    default: allow
    label: Health Connect
    methods:
      healthconnect.query:
        label: Read daily metrics, sleep, and workouts from Health Connect
      healthconnect.status:
        label: Check Health Connect sync status
  delete:
    default: ask
    label: Delete Health Connect data
    methods:
      healthconnect.delete:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Delete matching Health Connect records

# `get_file_content` and `get_file_preview` return full file bytes — the data
# the consent-gated "Download files" toggle governs — so content.read is a

# product follow-up. List/search/details reads carry file metadata only and
# always proceed.

# The `account-insights` command is keyed to interactions.read but returns the
# account-level insight metrics "Access analytics" (analytics.read) governs, so
# interactions.read is an account-insights DATA CLASS emitter. While analytics
# is deny/ask, Sentinel attaches an exclusion constraint and the trusted worker
# refuses the account-insights command; other interaction reads (followers,
# comments, likes) carry no insight metrics and proceed.
data_classes:
  account_insights:
    governed_by: analytics.read
    emitted_by:
      - interactions.read
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    methods:
      content.read:
        label: Access content
        approval_phrase: access content on Instagram
      interactions.read:
        label: Access followers, comments, likes, and insights
        approval_phrase: access your followers, comments, likes, and insights on Instagram
      analytics.read:
        label: Access analytics
        approval_phrase: access Instagram analytics
      algorithm.read:
        label: Access your algorithm
        approval_phrase: access your Instagram algorithm
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write and delete permissions
    methods:
      profile.edit:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Edit your profile
        approval_phrase: edit your Instagram profile
      algorithm.update:
        default: allow
        label: Edit your algorithm
        approval_phrase: update your Instagram algorithm
      content.post:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Post and edit content
        approval_phrase: post or edit your content on Instagram
      settings.edit:
        # Account settings such as muting change what the user sees; reusable consent stays task-scoped.
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Change your settings
        approval_phrase: change your Instagram settings
      saved.write:
        default: allow
        label: Manage saved posts
        approval_phrase: manage your saved posts on Instagram

# Slack message searches return message content even though they authorize
# under a separate Search Slack permission. Reaction reads can carry the
# message they describe. The worker refuses these whole while message reads
# are not allowed; the MCP response has no trusted partial projection.

    governed_by: messages.read
    emitted_by:
      - search.read
      - reactions.read

  burst: 10

    response_guard: verification_codes
    methods:
      mcp.read:
        label: Read GitHub repository content
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write permissions
    response_guard: verification_codes

      mcp.write:

        label: Change GitHub repository content
        approval_phrase: perform this action on your GitHub account
commands:

  install-url: null
  authorize-url: null
  exchange-code: null
  disconnect: null
  list-tools: mcp.read
  # Read calls are limited to a local, reviewed allowlist. GitHub's live
  # catalogue supplies names and schemas, never the permission decision.
  call-read-tool: mcp.read
  # New, unknown, and mutating tools intentionally remain on the write-capable
  # path so a provider catalogue change cannot silently widen access.
  call-tool: mcp.write
  # Packages caller-selected local files for GitHub's advertised push_files
  # tool without exposing the connector credential to git or the shell.
  push-files: mcp.write

connector: moltbook
display_name: Moltbook

icon: moltbook
request_quota:
  mode: shadow
  workers: [moltbook]
  queries_per_minute: 60
  burst: 10
cli:
  binary: moltbook
  skill: moltbook
  service: moltbook
# Moltbook consent scopes this client may request. Every connection asks for
# both. `offline_access` is always requested and is not a permission.
declarable_scopes:
  - read
  - write
# The CLI selects an exact compiled method; Moltbook enforces the operation's
# class, and Sentinel applies the user's policy. Keep method names distinct
# from group names so group-level Deny covers every method.
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    methods:
      # No `response_guard`, as in the other remote-MCP connectors: reads
      # return other agents' content, which SKILL.md marks untrusted.
      content.read:
        label: Read the agent's profile, home, channels, posts, comments, documents and field notes
        approval_phrase: read content in Moltbook
        requires_scopes:
          - read
      grants.read:
        label: See which capability grants the agent holds
        approval_phrase: read the agent's capability grants in Moltbook
        requires_scopes:
          - read
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write permissions
    methods:
      content.write:
        label: Post, comment, react, follow, keep field notes, edit the agent's profile, and manage channels and documents
        approval_phrase: post or change content in Moltbook
        requires_scopes:
          - write
      grants.write:
        label: Request or give up the agent's capability grants
        approval_phrase: change the agent's capability grants in Moltbook
        requires_scopes:
          - write
  delete:
    default: ask
    label: Delete permissions
    methods:
      content.delete:
        label: Delete posts, comments, documents and field notes
        approval_phrase: delete content in Moltbook
        requires_scopes:
          - write
commands:
  status: null
  authorize-url: null
  exchange-code: null
  refresh: null
  disconnect: null
  list-tools: content.read
  # Each Moltbook meta-tool selects its own method at runtime;
  # `execute_capability` selects the method its `permission` declares.
  call-tool: null
consent_screen:
  bullets:
    - icon: data_types
      title: "Join Moltbook as your Muse"
      text: "Muse will access things like the agent's profile, channels, posts, comments and documents on Moltbook."
    - icon: info
      title: "You choose what Muse can do"
      text: "By default, Muse will ask before posting, commenting or changing anything. Disconnect at any time in Settings."
    - icon: caution
      title: "Keep an eye on things"
      text: "Other agents' posts are untrusted content. Muse may take unexpected actions; monitor it carefully."
  footer_paragraphs:

  queries_per_minute: 180
  burst: 50
# The AI-search and database/meeting-notes query tools are keyed to
# search.read but return page and database CONTENT "Access content"
# (pages.read) governs, so search.read is a page-content DATA CLASS emitter.
# While pages.read is deny/ask, Sentinel attaches an exclusion constraint and
# the trusted worker refuses those content-bearing tools; plain search returns
# titles and links and proceeds.
data_classes:
  page_content:
    governed_by: pages.read
    emitted_by:
      - search.read
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    methods:
      pages.read:
        label: Access content
        approval_phrase: read your Notion pages and files
      search.read:
        label: Search content
        approval_phrase: search your Notion workspace
      workspace.read:
        label: Access workspace info
        approval_phrase: read workspace info like teams, members and comments
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write and delete permissions
    methods:
      pages.write:
        label: Create and edit content
        approval_phrase: create, edit, move or duplicate pages and folders in Notion
        approval_button_variants:
          create:
            allow_once: "Create"
            deny: "Don't create"
            deny.task: "Don't create"
          edit:
            allow_once: "Edit"
            deny: "Don't edit"
            deny.task: "Don't edit"
          move:
            allow_once: "Move"
            deny: "Don't move"
            deny.task: "Don't move"
          duplicate:
            allow_once: "Duplicate"
            deny: "Don't duplicate"
            deny.task: "Don't duplicate"
      comments.write:
        label: Add comments
        approval_phrase: post a comment in Notion
        approval_buttons:
          allow_once: "Comment"
          deny: "Don't comment"
          deny.task: "Don't comment"
      databases.write:
        label: Create and edit databases
        approval_phrase: create or change a database in Notion
        approval_button_variants:
          create:
            allow_once: "Create"
            deny: "Don't create"
            deny.task: "Don't create"
          edit:

    label: Read permissions
    # No verification-code guard. Arlo returns device names, models, status and
    # an image file, never a one-time code, so the guard would scan for a class
    # of secret this connector cannot emit. It is also incompatible with
    # `latest-image`: the framework rejects a declared output file on any

    methods:

      # either name, and do not declare them until the capability ships.
      devices.read:
        label: Read Arlo devices and connection status
      camera_snapshot.read:
        label: View the latest image from an Arlo camera

  skill: outlook_calendar

  backend: oauth
  credential_namespace: oauth_accounts
  domains:
    - graph.microsoft.com
  expose_routing_metadata: true

  mode: enforce
  workers:
    - outlook-calendar
  queries_per_minute: 600
  burst: 50
# Graph list rows carry organizer and the full attendee roster — event details
# "Access events" governs — so the list method is an event-details DATA CLASS
# emitter. While "Access events" is deny/ask, Sentinel attaches an exclusion
# constraint to allowed list calls and the trusted worker strips organizer and
# attendees from every returned row; subject, times, and location remain the
# "Show events" promise.
data_classes:
  event_details:
    governed_by: events.get
    emitted_by:
      - events.list
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    methods:
      events.list:
        label: Show events
        approval_phrase: view events in Outlook Calendar
      events.get:
        label: Access events
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write and delete permissions
    methods:
      events.create:
        default: allow
        label: Create events
      events.create_with_invitations:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Create events with invitations
        approval_phrase: create this event with invitations in Outlook Calendar
      events.update:
        default: allow
        label: Edit events
      events.update_with_notifications:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Update events and notify guests
        approval_phrase: update this event and notify guests in Outlook Calendar
      events.delete:
        default: allow
        label: Delete events
commands:
  status: null
  accounts: null
  disconnect: null
  authorize-url: null
  exchange-code: null
  list: events.list
  get: events.get
  # events.create_with_invitations instead when an --attendee is given.
  create: events.create
  # Reads the event first (events.get), then needs
  # events.update_with_notifications instead when you organize it and it has
  # attendees, or when its organizer, attendees or etag cannot be read.
  update: events.update
  delete: events.delete

# Graph list/search responses carry `bodyPreview` — a slice of the message
# content "Access messages" governs — so the list method is a message-content
# DATA CLASS emitter. While "Access messages" is deny/ask, Sentinel attaches
# an exclusion constraint to allowed list/search calls and the trusted worker
# strips the preview slice from every returned row.
data_classes:
  message_content:
    governed_by: messages.get
    emitted_by:
      - messages.list

    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    response_guard: verification_codes

  required: true

category: productivity
display_name: Tailscale
description: Securely connect to the devices on your private network
icon: tailscale
actions:
  auth:
    default: ask
    label: Join or leave a Tailscale network
    methods:
      tailscale.up:
        label: Join a Tailscale network
      # Split from tailscale.up because the argument decides WHOSE network
      # this device joins: a standing allow on the plain join must never
      # cover a join that hands the node to a different control server.
      # The manifest policy blocks persistent Allow. Task-scoped Allow also
      # requires a write action, so this auth method remains single-use-or-deny.
      tailscale.up_login_server:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_or_task_allow
        label: Join via a custom control server
        approval_phrase: join a Tailscale network via a custom control server
      tailscale.down:
        label: Leave and forget this device
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read Tailscale status
    methods:
      tailscale.status:
        label: Connection status and devices
        approval_phrase: view connection status and devices in Tailscale
      tailscale.ip:
        label: This device's Tailscale addresses
        approval_phrase: view this device's Tailscale addresses
commands:
  up: tailscale.up
  down: tailscale.down
  status: tailscale.status
  ip: tailscale.ip

icon: tessie
request_quota:
  mode: enforce
  workers:
    - tessie-api
  queries_per_minute: 300
  burst: 50
# Raw GET requests reach the same Tessie origin that serves vehicle status and
# location, so `api.read` is a vehicle-status DATA CLASS emitter. While "View
# vehicle status and location" is deny/ask, Sentinel attaches an exclusion
# constraint and the trusted worker refuses raw reads whole: a path-level
# projection cannot prove a raw read avoids the class.
data_classes:
  vehicle_status:
    governed_by: vehicles.list
    emitted_by:
      - api.read
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    methods:
      vehicles.list:
        label: View vehicle status and location
        approval_phrase: view vehicle status and location in Tessie
      api.read:
        label: View other vehicle data through Tessie API
        approval_phrase: view other vehicle data through the Tessie API
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write and delete permissions
    methods:
      vehicle.command.other:
        label: Control your vehicle
        approval_phrase: control your vehicle through Tessie
      vehicle.honk:
        default: allow
        label: Honk the horn
        approval_phrase: honk your vehicle through Tessie
      vehicle.flash_lights:
        default: allow
        label: Flash the lights
        approval_phrase: flash your vehicle's lights through Tessie
      vehicle.boombox:
        default: allow
        label: Play Boombox audio
        approval_phrase: play Boombox audio through Tessie
      vehicle.software_update:
        default: allow
        label: Schedule or cancel a software update
        approval_phrase: schedule or cancel a vehicle software update through Tessie
      telemetry.configure:
        default: allow
        label: Configure vehicle telemetry
        approval_phrase: configure vehicle telemetry through Tessie
      api.write.other:
        label: Make other changes through Tessie API
        approval_phrase: make other changes through the Tessie API
commands:
  authorize-url: null
  set-token: null
  disconnect: null
  verify: null
  vehicles: vehicles.list
  status: vehicles.list
  state: vehicles.list
  # vehicle.honk (honk), vehicle.flash_lights (flash), vehicle.boombox
  # (remote_boombox) or vehicle.software_update (schedule_software_update,
  # cancel_software_update) instead for those --command values.
  command: vehicle.command.other
  # api.read instead for `--method GET`; telemetry.configure instead for the
  # fleet_telemetry_config --path.
  request: api.write.other

    - icon: location
      title: "Share delivery details with Uber"
      text: "Muse sends pickup and drop-off locations, delivery instructions and recipient details to Uber for your delivery."
    - icon: data_types
      title: "Use your Uber account"
      text: "Muse reads your profile, available payment methods, Courier quotes, orders and driver tracking details."
    - icon: caution
      title: "Review bookings and cancellations"
      text: "When Connector approvals are enabled, Muse asks before booking and charging your selected Uber payment method, or cancelling an order. Prices can change, and cancellations may incur a fee."
  footer_paragraphs:
    - "The info used for your tasks is part of your interactions with Muse, which we may use to improve AI at Meta. You can manage this in Settings. Learn more in our [Privacy Policy](https://www.facebook.com/privacy/policy/)."
    - "Muse exchanges information with Uber. Review Uber's terms and privacy notice before connecting. Disconnect removes Muse's stored grant; manage provider-side access in Uber."
version: 1
metadata:
  visible_in_settings: false
auth: none
cli:
  binary: poppy
  skill: poppy
connector: poppy
display_name: Personal Agent Protocol
description: Work with companies through the Personal Agent Protocol
actions:
  read:
    # Reads of a verified company's protocol documents and declared GET
    # operations. The tool enforces the destination itself: only the company
    # it verified, its declared servers, public HTTPS addresses, no redirects.
    default: allow
    label: Read from companies
    methods:
      company.read:
        label: Read a company's protocol documents and operations
      api.read:
        label: Read from a company's API
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Act with companies
    methods:
      # Tool-composed protocol requests: no user data or model-written content.
      session.open:
        default: allow
        label: Start a session with a company
      # A declared operation with no files, such as proposing an order. Like
      # Shopify UCP's pre-purchase writes, it changes nothing the user has to
      # decide on: anything with an effect comes back as an operation that
      # needs operations.confirm. Set this to ask to review every write.
      api.write:
        default: allow
        label: Send a request to a company's API
      # Operations v2 has no cancel request, so the tool no longer uses this
      # method; it stays declared because removing a shipped key needs a
      # permanent retire migration.
      operations.cancel:
        default: allow
        label: Cancel a company's proposal
      # Signing the user in to their account at a company. The card shows the
      # company's checked sign-in link and code; nothing else does.
      account.connect:
        label: Sign in to your account at a company
        approval_phrase: sign in to your account
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        # No `approval_buttons`: clients that open the card's allow link
        # (`opens_url`) label the allow choice "Sign in" themselves; older
        # clients keep the standard Allow and the "Sign in at" link row.
      # Muse's own browser joining its session with a company (spec 0.3
      # section 6): the code comes from the page Muse's browser loaded, and
      # Sentinel allows this method only in the daemon's browser-join
      # context, never from a model-run command.
      browser_session.authorize:
        default: allow
        label: Use your session with a company in Muse's browser
      # Revoking Muse's access only reduces what Muse holds.
      account.disconnect:
        default: allow
        label: Disconnect your account from a company
      # The user's files: one card per upload, showing every file.
      api.upload:
        label: Send your files to a company
        approval_phrase: send your files
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
      # Operations v2 requires the user's approval of each exact revision, so
      # every confirmation is a single-use card showing the company's terms;
      # only then does the tool resend the request with `Poppy-Approval`.
      operations.confirm:
        label: Confirm an action a company proposed
        approval_phrase: confirm this proposal
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
commands:
  discover: company.read
  probe: company.read
  "session open": session.open
  "api describe": company.read
  # The method depends on the request: api.read, api.write, or api.upload.
  "api call": null
  "api upload": api.upload
  "operation confirm": operations.confirm
  "account connect": account.connect
  "account disconnect": account.disconnect
  "browser-session authorize": browser_session.authorize
  # No network: reads the worker's own browser records.
  "browser-session status": null
  # No network: lists what Muse holds in authd.
  "account list": null
  # Records a direct sign-in's redirect. For a sign-in the user already
  # allowed on its card, it also finishes it: company.read to discover the
  # company again, session.open for the token request.
  "account callback": null
version: 1
metadata:
  visible_in_settings: true
auth:
  provider: whatsapp_business
  required: true
connector: whatsapp_business
category: social
display_name: WhatsApp Business
description: Manage your WhatsApp Business profile and catalog
icon: whatsapp
# Runs on the WhatsApp worker and the linked WhatsApp Companion session; there
# is no second companion. Connecting only records consent.
request_quota:
  workers:
    - wai-privsep
cli:
  binary: hatch_wai_cli
  skill: whatsapp_business
actions:
  read:
    default: allow
    label: Read permissions
    methods:
      business.profile.read:
        label: View business profile
        approval_phrase: view your WhatsApp Business profile
      business.catalog.read:
        label: View catalog
        approval_phrase: view the products and collections in your WhatsApp catalog
  write:
    default: ask
    label: Write permissions
    methods:
      business.auth:
        # Only gets WhatsApp an access token it keeps to itself; each business
        # operation still asks for its own permission.
        default: allow
        label: Sign in to WhatsApp Business
        approval_phrase: sign in to your WhatsApp Business account
      business.profile.update:
        # The profile is public: each edit needs its own approval.
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Edit business profile
        approval_phrase: change your WhatsApp Business profile
      business.profile.set_picture:
        # Sends a photo from the device, and every contact sees it: each change
        # needs its own approval.
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Change profile picture
        approval_phrase: change your WhatsApp Business profile picture to a photo from your device
      business.profile.remove_picture:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Remove profile picture
        approval_phrase: remove your WhatsApp Business profile picture
      business.profile.set_cover_photo:
        # Sends a photo from the device, and anyone who views the profile sees
        # it: each change needs its own approval.
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Change cover photo
        approval_phrase: change your WhatsApp Business cover photo to a photo from your device
      business.profile.remove_cover_photo:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Remove cover photo
        approval_phrase: remove your WhatsApp Business cover photo
      business.catalog.edit:
        # Customer-visible but reversible; one approval can cover a batch of
        # edits when the business always-allows it.
        label: Edit your catalog
        approval_phrase: change products or collections in your WhatsApp catalog
      business.catalog.upload_image:
        # Sends a local file off the device, so an instruction injected through
        # a message must not be able to pick the file unseen.
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Upload product images
        approval_phrase: upload an image from your device for your WhatsApp catalog
      business.catalog.edit_with_images:
        # A product add or edit that also sends photos or videos from the
        # device; fresh for the same reason as an upload, and one approval
        # covers both.
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Add or edit products with photos or videos
        approval_phrase: add or change a product in your WhatsApp catalog using photos or videos from your device
      business.catalog.delete:
        approval_policy: fresh_consent_only
        label: Delete catalog items
        approval_phrase: permanently delete products or collections from your WhatsApp catalog
commands:
  business.auth: business.auth
  business.profile.show: business.profile.read
  business.profile.update: business.profile.update
  business.profile.set-picture: business.profile.set_picture
  business.profile.remove-picture: business.profile.remove_picture
  business.profile.set-cover-photo: business.profile.set_cover_photo
  business.profile.remove-cover-photo: business.profile.remove_cover_photo
  business.catalog.products: business.catalog.read
  business.catalog.product-list: business.catalog.read
  business.catalog.product: business.catalog.read
  business.catalog.collections: business.catalog.read
  business.catalog.collection: business.catalog.read
  business.catalog.create-catalog: business.catalog.edit
  # With --image or --video, these two authorize
  # business.catalog.edit_with_images.
  business.catalog.add-product: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.edit-product: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.delete-products: business.catalog.delete
  business.catalog.set-product-visibility: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.create-collection: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.update-collection: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.update-collection-list: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.delete-collections: business.catalog.delete
  business.catalog.appeal-product: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.appeal-collection: business.catalog.edit
  business.catalog.upload-image: business.catalog.upload_image

      title: "Run your business on WhatsApp"
      text: "Muse can use your linked WhatsApp Business account to view and update your business profile and catalog."
    - icon: info

      text: "WhatsApp Business works through the WhatsApp account you linked to Muse. Connect WhatsApp too if you haven't yet."

      title: "Keep an eye on things"
      text: "By default, Muse will ask before taking actions it thinks need review. You can change this in Settings, and disconnect at any time."
  footer_paragraphs:
    - "The info used for your tasks is part of your interactions with Muse, which we may use to improve AI at Meta. You can manage this in Settings. [Learn more](https://www.facebook.com/privacy/genai/)"
    - "Muse will exchange info with WhatsApp. To learn more, view their [business terms](https://www.whatsapp.com/legal/business-terms) and [privacy policy](https://www.whatsapp.com/legal/privacy-policy)."
